\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(

uv_{i+1})\}\in E(d_{f_{1}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
. Observe that, from Observation
58
, for every
uv_{q}\in V(d_{f}^{*},

\mathsf{Common}(f,

f_{1}))\cap V^{*}
,
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f}^{*},d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(uv_{q})\neq\mathsf{Null}
. Now, since
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f}^{*}}(uv_{q})\neq\mathsf{Null}
and
uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\in V(f,\mathsf{Common}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}}))\cap V^{*}
, we get that
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(uv_{i})\neq\mathsf{Null}
and
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(

uv_{i+1})\neq\mathsf{Null}

Condition
4
.
The edge
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
has
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
turning points in
f_{1}(c)
other than
(d(uv_{i}),\{u,v\})
and
(d(uv_{i+1}),\{u,v\})
(which may not be turning points in
f_{1}(c)
). We further discuss additional constraints satisfied in this case later.

Definition 63

(
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},

\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame. Let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},

E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
exhibits
Equality of Common Parts
with respect to
F
. Let
d_{f}^{*},d_{f_{1}}^{*},d_{f_{2}}^{*}
be the result of the application of
\mathsf{MakeAllVerSet}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}},\mathsf{Common}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}}),\mathsf{Common}(d_{f},

d_{f_{2}}),\mathsf{Common}(

d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}))
(see Definition
57
). Then,
C
exhibits
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
with respect to
F
if for every
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E_{f}
such that
uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\in V(d_{f})
, exactly one of the following four conditions holds:

1.

\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

2.

\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

3.

\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E(d_{f_{1}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

4.

\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
partly intersects
c
(see Definition
64
).

We now consider the case of Condition
4
of Definition
63
.
The edge
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
has
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
turning points in
f_{1}(c)
other than
(d(uv_{i}),\{u,v\})
and
(d(uv_{i+1}),\{u,v\})
(which may not be turning points in
f_{1}(c)
).
These
\ell
turning points,
(p,\{u,v\})
, must be such that
p\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
, since the edge
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
is drawn strictly inside
f
except for both its endpoints (e.g., see the edge
\{uv_{1},uv_{2}\}
in Figure
20(b)
). Therefore,
\ell
vertices from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
belong to
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
, and they are connected by edges from
E^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
in a “path-like manner”. Further, the numbering of these vertices should match the order of appearance of the points corresponding to them from
uv_{i}
to
uv_{i+1}
. Let
r\in[2]
be such that
uv_{i}
is drawn on
f_{r}(c)
. We expect that the vertex
uv_{i}
is mapped to
uv_{j}
, for some
j\in\mathbb{N}
, in
d_{r}
, and the remaining
\ell
vertices from
V^{*}
on
c\setminus f
are numbered
j+1,\ldots,j+\ell
in
d_{r}
. Formally,
uv_{j+1},\ldots,uv_{j+\ell}\in V(d_{r},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f))
(e.g., see the edge
\{uv_{1},uv_{2}\}
in Figure
20(b)
and the addition of its turning points and their labeling in Figure
21(a)
). Furthermore, for every
0\leq t<\ell
we expect that the vertices
uv_{j+t}
and
uv_{j+t+1}
are connected with an edge. So, we demand that exactly one of the following condition is satisfied.

Condition
1
.
The edge between the vertices
uv_{j+t}
and
uv_{j+t+1}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
except for both its endpoints, and therefore this edge is in
E_{f_{1}}
. Now, if
r=2
, the labeling of the endpoints of this edge in
d_{f_{1}}
might be other than
uv_{j+t}
and
uv_{j+t+1}
. In this case, we need to use the vertex identification function of
d_{f_{2}}
and
d_{f_{1}}
on
uv_{j+t}
and
uv_{j+t+1}
. Recall that the aforementioned
\ell
vertices represent turning points of
\{u,v\}
(in
(f_{1}(c)
or
f_{2}(c)
). Since they are drawn in
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
, it is easy to see that a point in this set is a turning point of
\{u,v\}
in
f_{1}(c)
if and only if it is a turning point of
\{u,v\}
in
f_{2}(c)
. So, we get that there exists a vertex from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
drawn on a point
p\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
of
d_{f_{1}}
if and only if there exists a vertex from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
drawn on a point
p\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
of
d_{f_{2}}
. Now, since
r\in[2]
is unknown, we mention the two possible cases.

•

If
r=1
, then
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

•

if
r=2
, then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+t}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+t+1})\}\in E_{f_{1}}
(e.g., see the edge
\{uv_{1},uv_{2}\}
in
f_{1}(c)
in Figure
21(a)
).

Condition
2
.
This condition is symmetric to the first condition. Here, the edge connecting the vertices
uv_{j+t}
and
uv_{j+t+1}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
except for both its endpoints, and therefore this edge is in
E_{f_{2}}
. There are two cases:

•

If
r=2
, then
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

•

if
r=1
, then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+t}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+t+1})\}\in E_{f_{2}}
(e.g., see the edge
\{uv_{3},uv_{4}\}
in
f_{2}(c)
in Figure
21(a)
).

Condition
3
.
The edge between the vertices
uv_{j+t}
and
uv_{j+t+1}
is drawn on
c
, that is,
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E(d_{r},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
(e.g., see the edge
\{st_{1},st_{2}\}
in
f_{2}(c)
in Figure
21(a)
).

Conditions
4
,
5
and
6
.
Similarly, we have an edge between the vertices
uv_{j+\ell}
and
uv_{i+1}
. We state the main differences between the cases corresponding to this edge. Observe that
uv_{i+1}
might not be drawn on
f_{r}(c)
, so we cannot assume that
uv_{i+1}
is labeled
uv_{j+\ell+1}
, as expected (since there is an edge between
uv_{j+\ell}
and
uv_{i+1}
). So, assume without loss of generality that
r=1
and
uv_{i+1}
is not drawn on
f_{1}(c)
. Then, if the edge between
uv_{j+\ell}
and
uv_{i+1}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
, except at its endpoints, then the edge will appear in
E_{f_{2}}
. Now, we wish to get the labeling of the endpoints of this edge. For the vertex
uv_{j+\ell}
, which is drawn on
c
, we can get its labeling from the drawing
d_{f_{1}}
, that is,
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+\ell})
. As for the vertex
uv_{i+1}
, we can get its labeling from the drawing
f
, that is,
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})
. Now, assume that
r=1
and the edge between
uv_{j+\ell}
and
uv_{i+1}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
. Then, we expect that
uv_{i+1}
is labeled
uv_{j+\ell+1}
, as explained, and so
\{uv_{j+\ell},uv_{j+\ell+1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

Definition 64

(
Partial Intersection of
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E_{f}
and
c
).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},

U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame. Let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),

d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
exhibits
Equality of Common Parts
with respect to
F
. Let
d_{f}^{*},d_{f_{1}}^{*},d_{f_{2}}^{*}
be the result of the application of
\mathsf{MakeAllVerSet}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}},\mathsf{Common}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}}),

\mathsf{Common}(d_{f},d_{f_{2}}),\mathsf{Common}(

d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}))
(see Definition
57
). Let
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E_{f}
such that
uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\in V(d_{f})
. Then,
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
partly intersects
c
if the following condition holds.
There exist
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
r\in[2]
such that

\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d^{*}_{f_{r}}}(uv_{i})=uv_{j}
,
uv_{j+1},\ldots,uv_{j+\ell}\in V(d_{f_{r}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f))
and for every
0\leq t<\ell
, exactly one of Conditions
1
-
3
and exactly one of Conditions
4
-
6
are satisfied:

1.

If
r=1
, then
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
; else,
r=2
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+t}),

\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}

(uv_{j+t+1})\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

2.

If
r=2
, then
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
; else,
r=1
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+t}),

\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}

(uv_{j+t+1})\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

3.

\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E(d_{f_{r}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

4.

If
r=1
, then
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i+1})=uv_{j+\ell+1}
and
\{uv_{j+\ell},uv_{j+\ell+1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
; else,
r=2
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+\ell}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}

(uv_{i+1})\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

5.

If
r=2
, then
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})=uv_{j+\ell+1}
and
\{uv_{j+\ell},uv_{j+\ell+1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
; else,
r=1
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+\ell}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}

(uv_{i+1})\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

6.

\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{r}}}(uv_{i+1})=uv_{j+\ell+1}
and
\{uv_{j+\ell},uv_{j+\ell+1}\}\in E(d_{f_{r}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

We proceed with other properties related to
partition of
E_{f}
. Specifically, the next two properties are similar to
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
, but with a few differences. Let
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E_{f}
such that
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
is an edge that is drawn strictly inside
f
, except at exactly one endpoint. Recall that since
F
is an info-frame, every vertex that is drawn strictly inside
f
is a vertex from the set
V
. So, in this case, any vertex that is not drawn on
f
, but drawn strictly inside
f
, must be a vertex from
V
and not from
V^{*}
. Therefore, we have two cases: either
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}=\{u,uv_{1}\}
(e.g., see the edge
\{u,uv_{1}\}
in Figure
20(b)
), or
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}=\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}
(e.g., see the edge
\{st_{3},t\}
in Figure
20(b)
). We handle the first case in the
Partition of
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
property (see Definition
65
), and the second case in the
Partition of
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
property (see Definition
67
). In what follows, we explain the differences between
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
and
Partition of
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
. (The differences between
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
and
Partition of
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
are similar). We first compare Definition
63
to Definition
65
.

Conditions
1
,
2
and
3
.
These conditions are similar to Conditions
1
,
2
and
3
of the definition of
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
(Definition
63
).

Condition
4
.

This condition is similar to Condition
4
of Definition
63
. Here, we consider the case where there is a turning point
(p,\{u,v\})
for
p\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
, other than the endpoints of
\{u,uv_{1}\}
, in
f_{1}(c)
(and
f_{2}(c)
). We further discuss additional constraints satisfied in this case later.

Definition 65

(
Partition of
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame. Let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
exhibits
Equality of Common Parts
with respect to
F
. Let
d_{f}^{*},d_{f_{1}}^{*},d_{f_{2}}^{*}
be the result of the application of
\mathsf{MakeAllVerSet}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}},\mathsf{Common}

(d_{f},d_{f_{1}}),

\mathsf{Common}

(d_{f},d_{f_{2}}),\mathsf{Common}(d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}))
(see Definition
57
). Then,
C
exhibits
Partition of
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
with respect to
F
if for every
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
such that
u\in U_{f}
, exactly one of the following four conditions holds:

1.

\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

2.

\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

3.

\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E(d_{f_{1}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

4.

\{u,uv_{1}\}
partly intersects
c
(see Definition
66
).

We now consider the case of Condition
4
of Definition
65
. Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
such that there are
\ell
turning points
(p,\{u,v\})
for
p\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
, other than the endpoints of
\{u,uv_{1}\}
, in each of
f_{1}(c)
and
f_{2}(c)
. Recall that the purpose of the numbering of vertices from the set
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
is to state the order of the appearance of the vertices, from
u
to
v
. Now, these vertices (that are from the set
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
) that are on
c
, appear before the intersection of the edge
\{u,v\}
and
f
(represented by the vertex
uv_{1}
in
d_{f}
). Therefore, we expect that the numbering of these vertices will be
1
to
\ell
, where
\ell
is the number of vertices from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
in
f_{1}(c)
(observe that
\ell
is also the number of vertices from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
in
f_{2}(c)
). So, as opposed to Condition
4
of the definition of
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
(Definition
63
), the numbering of vertices from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
is known in advance.

Conditions
1
,
2
and
3
.
First, we consider the edges
\{uv_{t},uv_{t+1}\}
for every
1\leq t\leq\ell
. Here, the cases are similar to Conditions
1
,
2
and
3
of the definition of
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
(Definition
63
).

Next, we consider the edge between
uv_{1}
and
u
. We have that exactly one of Conditions
4
,
5
,
6
,
7
and
8
is satisfied.

Condition
4
.
The vertex
u
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
, and therefore we have that
u\in U_{f_{1}}
. In addition, since
uv_{1}
is the vertex from the set
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
that is the closest to
u
, it follows that the edge
\{u,uv_{1}\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
except at the endpoint
d(uv_{1})
. So,
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

Condition
5
.
Similarly, the vertex
u
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
, and therefore we have that
u\in U_{f_{2}}
. In addition, since
uv_{1}
is the vertex from the set
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
that is the closest to
u
, then, it follows that the edge
\{u,uv_{1}\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
except at the endpoint
d(uv_{1})
. So,
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
(e.g., see the vertex
t
and the edge
\{st_{3},t\}
in Figure
21(a)
).

Condition
6
.
The vertex
u
is drawn on
c
, so
u\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))
, and the edge
\{u,uv_{1}\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
except at its endpoints. Then,
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

Condition
7
.
The vertex
u
is drawn on
c
, so
u\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))
, and the edge
\{u,uv_{1}\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
except at its endpoints. Then,
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

Condition
8
.
The vertex
u
and the edge
\{u,uv_{1}\}
are drawn on
c
. So,
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E(d_{f_{1}}^{*},

\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

Definition 66

(
Partial Intersection of
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
and
c
).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},

\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame. Let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},

E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
exhibits
Equality of Common Parts
with respect to
F
. Let
d_{f}^{*},d_{f_{1}}^{*},d_{f_{2}}^{*}
be the result of the application of
\mathsf{MakeAllVerSet}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}},\mathsf{Common}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}}),\mathsf{Common}(d_{f},

d_{f_{2}}),\mathsf{Common}(d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}))
(see Definition
57
). Then,
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
such that
u\in U_{f}
, partly intersects
c
if the following condition holds.
There exist
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
such that
uv_{1},\ldots,uv_{\ell}\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f))
, and for every
1\leq t\leq\ell
, exactly one of Conditions
1
-
3
and exactly one of Conditions
4
-
6
are satisfied:

1.

\{uv_{t},uv_{t+1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

2.

\{uv_{t},uv_{t+1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

3.

\{uv_{t},uv_{t+1}\}\in E(d_{f_{1}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

4.

u\in U_{f_{1}}
and
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

5.

u\in U_{f_{2}}
and
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

6.

u\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))
and
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

7.

u\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))
and
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

8.

\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E(d_{f_{1}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

We now present a similar case to Definitions
65
and
66
, where the edge we examine is
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}
.

Definition 67

(
Partition of
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame. Let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
exhibits
Equality of Common Parts
with respect to
F
. Let
d_{f}^{*},d_{f_{1}}^{*},d_{f_{2}}^{*}
be the result of the application of
\mathsf{MakeAllVerSet}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}},\mathsf{Common}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}}),

\mathsf{Common}(d_{f},d_{f_{2}}),\mathsf{Common}(d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}))
(see Definition
57
). Then,
C
exhibits
Partition of

\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},

v\}\in E_{f}

With One Endpoint on
f
with respect to
F
if for every
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},

v\}\in E_{f}
such that
v\in U_{f}
, exactly one of the following four conditions holds:

1.

\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)}),v\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

2.

\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)}),v\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

3.

\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)}),v)\}\in E(d_{f_{1}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

4.

\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}
partly intersects
c
(see Definition
68
).

Definition 68

(
Partial Intersection of
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\in E_{f}
and
c
).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},

U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame. Let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},

E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
exhibits
Equality of Common Parts
with respect to
F
. Let
d_{f}^{*},d_{f_{1}}^{*},d_{f_{2}}^{*}
be the result of the application of
\mathsf{MakeAllVerSet}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}},

\mathsf{Common}(d_{f},d_{f_{1}}),

mathsf{Common}(d_{f},d_{f_{2}}),\mathsf{Common}(

d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}))
(see Definition
57
). Let
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\in E_{f}
such that
v\in U_{f}
. Then,
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}
partly intersects
c
if the following condition holds.
There exist
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
r\in[2]
such that
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d^{*}_{f_{r}}}(uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)})=uv_{j},uv_{j+1},\ldots,uv_{j+\ell}\in V(d_{f_{r}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f))
, and for every
0\leq t<\ell
, exactly one of Conditions
1
-
3
and exactly one of Conditions
4
-
8
are satisfied:

1.

If
r=1
, then
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
; else,
r=2
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+t}),

\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}

(uv_{j+t+1})\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

2.

If
r=2
, then
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
; else,
r=1
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+t}),

\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}

(uv_{j+t+1})\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

3.

\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E(d_{f_{r}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

4.

v\in U_{f_{1}}
. If
r=1
, then
\{uv_{j+\ell},v\}\in E_{f_{1}}
; else,
r=2
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+\ell}),v\}

\in E_{f_{1}}
.

5.

v\in U_{f_{2}}
.If
r=2
, then
\{uv_{j+\ell},v\}\in E_{f_{2}}
; else,
r=1
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+\ell}),v\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

6.

v\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))
. If
r=1
, then
\{uv_{j+\ell},v\}\in E_{f_{1}}
; else,
r=2
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+\ell}),v\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

7.

v\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))
. If
r=2
, then
\{uv_{j+\ell},v\}\in E_{f_{2}}
; else,
r=1
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+\ell}),v\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

8.

\{uv_{j+\ell},v\}\in E(d_{f_{r}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

We continue with the last property. Here, we consider the edges drawn strictly inside
f
. That is, edges of the form
\{u,v\}\in E
where
u,v\in U_{f}
and
V(d_{f})\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
(e.g., see the edge
\{x,w\}
in Figure
20(b)
). Recall, that
u,v\in U_{f}
means that
u
and
v
are drawn strictly inside
f
, and considering that also
V(d_{f})\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
, we have that the edge
\{u,v\}\in E
does not intersect
f
; therefore, the edge
\{u,v\}
is drawn strictly inside
f
.
So, let
\{u,v\}\in E
be such an edge. We expect that exactly one of the following conditions is satisfied.

Condition
1
.
The edge
\{u,v\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
. Therefore, the vertices
u
and
v
are drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
, so
u,v\in U_{f_{1}}
. In addition, in this case, since
\{u,v\}
does not intersect
f_{1}(c)
, and, in particular, it does not intersect
c
, it follows that
V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
.

Condition
2
.
Similarly to the previous condition, the edge
\{u,v\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
. Therefore, the vertices
u
and
v
are drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
, so
u,v\in U_{f_{2}}
. In addition, in this case, since
\{u,v\}
does not intersect
f_{2}(c)
, and, in particular, it does not intersect
c
, it follows that
V(d_{f_{2}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
.

Condition
3
.
The edge
\{u,v\}
is drawn on
c
. Observe that in this case, there are no turning points of
\{u,v\}
and
f_{1}(c)
except at the endpoints, so
V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
.

Condition
4
.

This condition is similar to Condition
4
of Definition
63
. Here, we consider the case where there is a turning point
(p,\{u,v\})
for
p\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
, other than the endpoints of
\{u,v\}
, in both
f_{1}(c)
and
f_{2}(c)
. We further discuss additional constraints satisfied in this case later.

Definition 69

(
Partition of
\{u,v\}\in E
Drawn Strictly Inside
f
).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},

\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame. Then,
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),

F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},

E_{f_{2}},

U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
exhibits
Partition of
\{u,v\}\in E
Drawn Strictly Inside
f
with respect to

if for every
u,v\in U_{f}
such that
\{u,v\}\in E
and

, exactly one of the following four conditions holds:

1.

and
u,v\in U_{f_{1}}
.

2.

V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset

3.

V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset

\{u,v\}
partly intersects
c
(see Definition

).

We now consider the case of Condition
4
of Definition
69
. Observe that the previous three conditions of Definition
69
deal with the case where no vertices from the set
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
are created. That is, the edge
\{u,v\}
has no turning points in
f_{1}(c)
or
f_{2}(c)
, except maybe at the endpoints. Condition
4
deals with the other case, where we have at least one such a turning point. Observe that since
V(d_{f})\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
, the vertices from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
(that represent turning points of
\{u,v\}
in
f_{1}(c)
or
f_{2}(c)
) belong to
V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f))
(e.g., see the edge
\{x,w\}
in Figure
21(a)
). Therefore, there exist
uv_{1},\ldots uv_{\ell}\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f))
such that, for every
1\leq t<\ell
, exactly one of the following conditions is satisfied:

Condition
1
.
The edge between the vertices
uv_{j}
and
uv_{j+1}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
, except at the endpoints, and therefore,
\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
.

Condition
2
.
The edge between the vertices
uv_{j}
and
uv_{j+1}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
, except at the endpoints, and therefore,
\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
.

Condition
3
.
The edge between the vertices
uv_{j}
and
uv_{j+1}
is drawn on
c
, so
\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}\in E(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
.

Conditions
4
-
8
.

These conditions deal with the part of the edge that appears first, that is,
\{u,uv_{1}\}
, and are similar to Conditions
4
-

of Definition
65
.

Conditions

-
13
.

These conditions deal with the part of the edge that appears last, that is,
\{uv_{\ell},v\}
, and are similar to Conditions
4
-
8
of this definition.

Now, we are ready to define the term
info-cutter
of an info frame. A triple
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
is an info-cutter of an info-frame
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
if
C
exhibits all the properties defined in this subsection with respect to
F
. Formally, we have the following definition.

Definition 71

(
Info-Cutter).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame. Then
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
is an info-cutter of
F
if
C
exhibits the following properties with respect to
F
:

Info-Cutter Template
,

U_{f}
-Partitioned
,

Equality of Common Parts
,

Equality of
V^{*}
Directions
,

Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
,

Partition of
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
,

Partition of
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
and

Partition of
\{u,v\}\in E
Drawn Strictly Inside
f
.

Algorithm 3

\mathsf{Splitter}

The Function
\mathsf{SplitterPart}1
.
In Algorithm
3
, we define the function
\mathsf{Splitter}
and its output
C=(c,F_{1},F_{2})
,
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
.
We show that
C
is an info-cutter of
F
,
d_{1}
is a drawing of
F_{1}
and
d_{2}
is a drawing of
F_{2}
.
For this purpose, we define another function,
\mathsf{SplitterPart}1(F,d,c)
in Algorithm
2
(used by the function
\mathsf{Splitter}
) that returns
(d^{\prime}_{1},F(d^{\prime}_{1}))
, such that
F(d^{\prime}_{1})
is an info-frame, and
d^{\prime}_{1}
is a drawing of
F(d^{\prime}_{1})
. The function
\mathsf{Splitter}
also uses the function
\mathsf{SplitterPart}2
, which is identical to
\mathsf{SplitterPart}1
up to the obvious changes. Later, we will explain the steps of Algorithm
2
. First, we define the two functions used by
\mathsf{SplitterPart}1
.

The first function we define, called
\mathsf{DeleteVer}
, is the “reverse” operation of
\mathsf{MakeVer}
(see Definition
52
in Section
6.3
). Intuitively, this function delete an “unnecessary” vertex from
V^{*}
in a
G^{*}
-drawing
d
. That is, a vertex
uv_{i}\in V(d)\cap V^{*}
such that
(d(uv_{i}),\{u,v\})
is not a turning point in
f_{1}(c)
(e.g., see the vertex
st_{3}
in Figure
23(c)
, which is deleted in Figure
23(d)
). Observe that in this case,
\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i}\},\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E(d)
. We now define formally the function
\mathsf{DeleteVer}
, whose input is a
G^{*}
-drawing
d
and a vertex
uv_{i}\in V(d)\cap V^{*}
such that
\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i}\},\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E(d)
, and delete the vertex from
d
.
For two sequences of points
P=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{\ell})
, and
Q=(q_{1},\ldots,q_{k})
, where
p_{\ell}=q_{1}
, we denote by
P_{1}\cdot P_{2}
the sequence of points
(p_{1},\ldots,p_{\ell},q_{2},\ldots,q_{k})
.

Definition 72

(
\mathsf{DeleteVer}
).

Let
d
be a
G^{*}
-drawing and let
uv_{i}\in V(d)\cap V^{*}
such that
\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i}\},\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E(d)
. Then,
\mathsf{DeleteVer}(d,uv_{i})
performs the following steps on
d
:

•

Add the edge
\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i+1}\}
to
E(d)
, and update
d(\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i+1}\})=d(\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i}\})\cdot d(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
.

•

Delete the edges
\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i}\}
and
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
from
E(d)
.

•

For every
i<\ell\leq\mathsf{index}(u,v)
, rename
uv_{\ell}
to
uv_{\ell-1}
.

(d)

Next, we define the term
info-frame induced
by a
G^{*}
-drawing. Intuitively, given a
G^{*}
-drawing
d
and a frame
f
, we would like to compute the info-frame
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
such that
d
is a drawing of
F
(e.g., see Figures
23(d)
and
23(e)
). Observe that not for every
G^{*}
-drawing
d
and a frame
f
there exists such an info-frame, but if there exists, then it is unique. If
d
is not bounded by
f
, or if there is a vertex
uv_{i}\in V(d)\cap V^{*}
drawn strictly inside
f
, then there is no aforementioned info-frame. In addition, recall that we demand
d=^{\mathsf{PlanePoints}(f)}d_{f}
(see Definition
49
in Section
6.2
). Now, observe that for every drawing
d_{f}
on
f
, and for every turning point
(p,\{u,v\})
in
f
in
d_{f}
, there exists
uv_{i}\in V(d_{f})\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
such that
d_{f}(uv_{i})=p
. So, since
d=^{\mathsf{PlanePoints}(f)}d_{f}
, we get that
d(uv_{i})=p
. We conclude that for every turning point
(p,\{u,v\})
in
f
in
d
, there exists
uv_{i}\in V(d)\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
such that
d(uv_{i})=p
. Now, recall that for every
uv_{i}\in V(d_{f_{1}})\cap V^{*}
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
there are no edges on
f
attached to it, and
uv_{i}
has exactly one neighbor.
Formally, we define the term info-frame induced by a
G^{*}
-drawing as follows.

Definition 73

(
Frame Induced By a
G^{*}
-Drawing).

Let
d
be a
G^{*}
-drawing, and let
f
be a frame such that the following conditions are satisfied:

1.

d
is bounded by
f
.

2.

For every turning point
(p,\{u,v\})
in
f
in
d
, there exists
uv_{i}\in V(d)\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
such that
d(uv_{i})=p
.

3.

For every
uv_{i}\in V(d)\cap V^{*}
,
d(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
.

4.

For every
uv_{i}\in V(d_{f_{1}})\cap V^{*}
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(

f_{\mathsf{init}})
there exists exactly one
z\in\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i+1}\}
such that
\{z,uv_{i}\}\in E(d)
. In addition,
\mathsf{PlanePoints}

(d(\{z,uv_{i}\}))\cap\mathsf{PlanePoints}(f)\subseteq\{d(uv_{i}),d(z)\}
.

Then, the info-frame induced by
d
and
f
is
\mathsf{IndInfFra}(d,f)=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
, where:

1.

d_{f}
is defined as follows:
V(d_{f})=V(d,\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
,
E(d_{f})=E(d,\mathsf{PlanePoints}(

f))
. For every
u\in V(d_{f})
,
d_{f}(u)=d(u)
, and for every
e\in E(d_{f})
,
d_{f}(e)=d(e)
.

2.

E_{f}\subseteq E(d)
is the set of edges drawn strictly inside
f
in
d
except for at least one of their endpoints, which is drawn on
f
.

3.

U_{f}
is the set of vertices drawn strictly inside
f
in
d
.

4.

For every
uv_{i}\in V(d_{f_{1}})\cap V^{*}
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(

f_{\mathsf{init}})
, let
z\in\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i+1}\}
such that
\{z,uv_{i}\}\in E(d)
. Let
E(d)=(d(uv_{i}),p_{1},\ldots,d(z))
. Then,
\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f}(uv_{i})=p_{1}
.

It is easy to see that
\mathsf{IndInfFra}(d,f)
satisfies the conditions of Definition
48
. Therefore,
\mathsf{IndInfFra}(d,f)
is an info-frame:

Observation 74
.

Let
d
be a
G^{*}
-drawing, and let
f
be a frame such that the conditions of Definition
73
are satisfied. Then,
\mathsf{IndInfFra}(d,f)
is an info-frame.

Later, we show that
d
is a drawing of
F
.
Now we are ready to describe the steps of Algorithm
2
.

Lines
2
-
2
: Turning Points in
f_{1}(c)
Turn to Vertices From
V^{*}
.
In the first part of the algorithm, we turn every turning point of every edge
\{u,v\}
in
f_{1}(c)
to a vertex from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
. In particular, for every
\{u,v\}\in E
we iterate over
0\leq i\leq\mathsf{index}(u,v)
such that
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E(d)
. Then, for every turning point
p
drawn on this edge, except at its endpoints, we add a vertex from the set
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
by activating
\mathsf{MakeVer}(d_{1},\{u,v\},p)
(see Definition
52
in Section
6.3
) (e.g., see the red vertices
st_{4}
,
xw_{1}
,
uv_{2}
,
uv_{3}
and
mn_{2}
in Figure
23(b)
). Recall that after activating
\mathsf{MakeVer}

d_{1}
remains
G^{*}
-drawing, due to
Observation
53
.

Lines
2
-
2
: Deleting Edges and Vertices Drawn Strictly Outside
f_{1}(c)
.
In these steps, we aim to obtain from
d_{1}
exactly the part of
d_{1}
that is bounded by
f_{1}(c)
. Observe that, after preforming Lines
2
-
2
, for each
e\in E(d_{1})
, exactly one of the following conditions holds:

•

e
is drawn inside
f_{1}(c)
.

•

e
is drawn strictly outside
f_{1}(c)
.

•

e
is drawn strictly outside
f_{1}(c)
except at least one of its endpoints, which is drawn on
f_{1}(c)
.

We delete every edge that satisfies the second or third conditions above. In addition, we delete every vertex drawn strictly outside
f_{1}(c)
in
d_{1}
(e.g., see Figures
23(b)
and
23(c)
).

Lines
2
-
2
: Deleting Unnecessary Vertices From
V^{*}
and Fixing Labelings.
Observe that at this stage of the algorithm, we have two issues with
d_{1}
: First, there might be some vertices from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
that are not drawn on a turning point of
\{u,v\}
in
f_{1}(c)
. Second, since we deleted vertices drawn strictly outside
f_{1}(c)
, the labeling of the vertices in
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
might not be continuous, for some
\{u,v\}\in E
. We fix these two issues simultaneously: We iterate over
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
, for each
\{u,v\}\in E
. If
uv_{i}
is indeed drawn on a turning point of
\{u,v\}
in
f_{1}(c)
, we fix its labeling (e.g., see the blue vertices in Figure
23(d)
); otherwise, we delete it, using
\mathsf{DeleteVer}
defined in Definition
72
(e.g., see the vertex
st_{3}
in Figure
23(c)
, which is deleted in Figure
23(d)
). Observe that every vertex in
V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}
at the beginning of the algorithm is drawn on
f
, since
d_{1}=d
and
d
is a drawing of
F
. After Lines
2
-
2
, we might add vertices to
V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}
drawn on
f_{1}(c)
. So, after these lines, each vertex in
V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}
is drawn on
f_{1}(c)
or on
f
. After Lines
2
-
2
, we delete every vertex drawn strictly outside
f_{1}(c)
. So, at this stage, every vertex in
V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}
is drawn on
f_{1}(c)
. Thus, if for some
uv_{i}\in V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}
,
(d_{1}(uv_{i}),\{u,v\})
is not a turning point in
f_{1}(c)
, then
\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i}\},\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i}\}\in E(d_{1})\cap E^{*}
. Therefore,
\mathsf{DeleteVer}(d_{1},uv_{i})
in Line
2
is well defined.

Line
2
: Computing the Induced Frame of
d_{1}
and
f_{1}(c)
.
Observe that at this stage of the algorithm we have the following conditions satisfied (where for the last condition, we present a proof below):

1.

d_{1}
is a
G^{*}
-drawing (see Lemma
76
) bounded by
f_{1}(c)
.

2.

Every turning point of every edge
\{u,v\}
in
f_{1}(c)
is a vertex from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
.

3.

Every vertex form
V(d)\cap V^{*}
is drawn on
f_{1}(c)
.

4.

For every
uv_{i}\in V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}
such that
d_{1}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(

f_{\mathsf{init}})
there exists exactly one
z\in\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i+1}\}
such that
\{z,uv_{i}\}\in E(d_{1})
. In addition,
\mathsf{PlanePoints}(d_{1}(\{z,uv_{i}\}))\cap\mathsf{PlanePoints}(f_{1}(c))\subseteq\{d_{1}(uv_{i}),d_{1}(z)\}
(see Lemma
75
).

We now show that Condition
4
is satisfied.

Lemma 75
.

For every
uv_{i}\in V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}
such that
d_{1}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
there exists exactly one
z\in\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i+1}\}
such that
\{z,uv_{i}\}\in E(d_{1})
. In addition,
\mathsf{PlanePoints}(d_{1}(\{z,uv_{i}\}))\cap\mathsf{PlanePoints}(f_{1}(c))\subseteq\{d_{1}(uv_{i}),d_{1}(z)\}
.

Proof.

Let
uv_{i}\in V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}
such that
d_{1}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(

f_{\mathsf{init}})
. First, assume that
d_{1}(uv_{i})
is on
f
. Observe that
d_{1}(uv_{i})
is not on
c
, so
d_{1}(uv_{i})
is not on
f_{2}(c)
. So, if there exists an edge with
uv_{i}
as one of its endpoints, on
f_{1}(c)
, then there exists one also on
f
, a contradiction to
F
being an info-frame (see Condition
1
of Definition
47
). Now, let
u_{j}=\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{1},d}(uv_{i})
. Since
F
is an info-frame, for exactly one
z^{\prime}
among
uv_{j-1}
and
uv_{j+1}
we get that
\{uv_{j},z^{\prime}\}\in d
(see Condition
1
of Definition
47
). Now, if there are no turning points of
\{uv_{j},z^{\prime}\}
in
f
in
d
, then
z=\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{1},d_{f_{2}}}(z^{\prime})
is the only neighbor of
uv_{i}
in
d_{1}
, and
z\in\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
since
d_{1}
is a
G^{*}
-drawing. Otherwise, there exists at least one turning point of
\{uv_{j},z^{\prime}\}
in
f
in
d
. Let
p
be the closest point to
uv_{j}
in the path
d(\{uv_{j},z^{\prime}\})
such that
(p,\{uv_{j},z^{\prime}\})
is a turning point in
f_{1}(c)
in
d
. So, there exists
z\in V(d_{1})\cap U^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
such that
d_{1}(z)=p
,
\{uv_{i},z\}\in E(d_{1})
and
z\in\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
, since
d_{1}
is a
G^{*}
-drawing. Observe that
z
is the only neighbor of
uv_{i}
in
d_{1}
. In addition, observe that
\{uv_{i},z\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
except for its endpoints, which are drawn inside
f
, so
\mathsf{PlanePoints}(d_{1}(\{z,uv_{i}\}))\cap\mathsf{PlanePoints}(f_{1}(c))\subseteq\{d_{1}(uv_{i}),d_{1}(z)\}
.

Second, assume that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})
is not on
f
. Then, it belongs to
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
. So, there exists
\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}\in E(d)
such that

d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}

(d(\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}))
and
(d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}),\{u,v\})
is a turning point in
f_{1}(c)
in
d
. Let
c=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{t})
and let
d(\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\})=(p_{1},\ldots p_{q})
. Let
a,b\in\mathbb{N}
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})
is on
\ell(c_{a},c_{a+1})
and on
\ell(p_{b},p_{b+1})
. Since
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
, then
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\neq c_{a},c_{a+1},p_{b},p_{b+1}
. So, exactly one among
\ell(p_{b},d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}))
and
\ell(d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}),p_{b+1})
is inside
f_{1}(c)
. Thus,
uv_{i}
has exactly one neighbor in
d_{1}
, and no edges on
f_{1}(c)
are attached to it. So, we get that there exists exactly one
z\in\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i+1}\}
such that
\{z,uv_{i}\}\in E(d_{1})
, and
\mathsf{PlanePoints}(d_{1}(\{z,uv_{i}\}))\cap\mathsf{PlanePoints}(f_{1}(c))\subseteq\{d_{1}(uv_{i}),d_{1}(z)\}
. This completes the proof.
∎

So, the conditions of Definition
73
are satisfied, and hence
\mathsf{IndInfFra}(d_{1},f_{1}(c))
is well defined. Moreover, by Observation
74
,
\mathsf{IndInfFra}(d_{1},f_{1}(c))
is an info-frame (e.g., see Figure
23(e)
).

The function
\mathsf{SplitterPart}_{1}(F,d,c)
returns
d_{1}
and
\mathsf{IndInfFra}(d_{1},f_{1}(c))
.

We now prove that
d_{1}
returned by Algorithm
2
, is indeed a drawing of
\mathsf{IndInfFra}(d_{1},f_{1}(c))
. To this end, we first prove that
d_{1}
is a
G^{*}
-drawing:

Lemma 76
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
d
be a drawing of
F
and let
c
be a cutter of
f
. Let
d_{1}
and
F_{1}
be the output of
\mathsf{SplitterPart}_{1}(F,d,c)
. Then,
d_{1}
is a
G^{*}
-drawing.

Proof.

We prove that
d_{1}
is a
G^{*}
-drawing by showing that the conditions of Definition
38
are satisfied. We first show that Condition
1
is satisfied, that is,
(V(d_{1}),E(d_{1}))
is valid, by showing that the Conditions of Definition
37
are satisfied:

1.

It is easy to see that
V(d_{1})\subseteq V\cup V^{*}
and
E(d_{1})\subseteq E\cup E^{*}
, so Condition
1
is satisfied.

2.

Let
\{u,v\}\in E
, and assume that
V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}\neq\emptyset
.

(a)

In Lines
2
-
2
the algorithm labels the vertices in
V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
from
1
to
\mathsf{index}(u,v)=|V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}|
, so Condition
2(b)i
is satisfied.

(b)

We aim to prove that
E(d_{1})\cap E^{*}_{\{u,v\}}\subseteq\{\{u,uv_{1}\}\}\cup\{\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}~|~1\leq j\leq\mathsf{index}(u,v)-1\}\cup\{\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\}
. We saw that
d_{1}
obtained by the end of Line
2
is a
G^{*}
-drawing, so Condition
2(b)ii
holds at this stage. In Lines
2
-
2
, we delete edges, so the condition still holds by the end of these steps. Now, in Lines
2
-
2
observe that we iterate over the vertices in
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
by the order of their labeling at the beginning of this stage. In every iteration, we either rename or delete the current vertex from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
. For every vertex we delete, we connect its two neighbors with an edge and we do not change the labeling of vertices we have already labeled. So, when we rename a vertex
v_{a_{1}}
, then the label of the previous vertex we renamed is
v_{a_{1}-1}
and it is the only vertex that may be connected with an edge to
v_{a_{1}}
among the vertices we have already labeled. Thus, by the end of the algorithm, Condition
2(b)ii
is satisfied.

(c)

We aim to show that Condition
2(b)iii
holds, that is,
\{u,v\}\notin E(d_{1})
. Assume towards a contradiction that
\{u,v\}\in E(d_{1})
. We saw that
d_{1}
obtained by the end of Line
2
is a
G^{*}
-drawing, so Condition
2(b)iii
holds in this stage. So,
\{u,v\}\notin E(d_{1})
by the end of Line
2
. Now, since
\{u,v\}\in E(d_{1})
by the end of the algorithm, this means that in Lines
2
-
2
we deleted every vertex in
V(d)\cap V_{\{u,v\}}^{*}
, a contradiction to the assumption that
V(d)\cap V_{\{u,v\}}^{*}\neq\emptyset
.

We saw that the conditions of Definition
37
are satisfied, so
(V(d_{1}),E(d_{1}))
is valid, and hence Condition
1
is satisfied.

Now, we aim to prove that Condition
2
is satisfied. Let
u\in V(d_{1})\cap V
. Observe that
d_{1}(u)=d(u)
, and since
d
is a
G^{*}
-drawing,
d(u)\in\mathsf{StrictGridPoints}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
. Let
uv_{i}\in V(d_{1})\cap V^{*}
. If
uv_{i}
is on
f
, then there exists
uv_{j}\in V(d)\cap V^{*}
such that

d_{1}(uv_{i})=d(uv_{j})
. So, since
d
is a
G^{*}
-drawing,
d(uv_{j})\in{\cal P}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
. If
d_{1}(uv_{i})
is not on
f
, then it belongs to
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
. So, there exists
\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}\in E(d)
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}

(d(\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}))
and
(d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}),\{u,v\})
is a turning point in
f_{1}(c)
in
d
. Let
c=(c_{1},\ldots,c_{t})
and let
d(\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\})=(p_{1},\ldots p_{q})
. Let
a,b\in\mathbb{N}
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})
is on
\ell(c_{a},c_{a+1})
and on
\ell(p_{b},p_{b+1})
. Now, observe that
d_{1}(uv_{i})\neq p_{b},p_{b+1}
, and since
d
is a
G^{*}
-drawing,
d(\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\})\in{\cal P}^{*}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
. Thus, there exist
x,y\in\mathsf{StrictGridPoints}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
such that
\ell(p_{b},p_{b+1})
is on
\ell(x,y)
. Therefore,
d_{1}(uv_{i})
is the intersection point of
\ell(x,y)
and
\ell(c_{a},c_{a+1})
, so
d_{1}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
. Thus, we get that Condition
2
is satisfied.

We now show that Condition
3
is satisfied.
Let
\{u,v\}\in E(d_{1})\cap E
. Observe that, in this case, there are no turning points of
\{u,v\}
in
f_{1}(c)
in
d
. Therefore,
\{u,v\}\in E(d)\cap E
,
d_{1}(\{u,v\})=d(\{u,v\})
, and since
d
is a
G^{*}
-drawing,
d(\{u,v\})\in{\cal P}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
. Now, let
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E(d)\cap E^{*}
. There exists
\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}\in V(d)
, where
d(\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\})=(p_{1},\ldots p_{q})
, and
1\leq a_{1}\leq a_{2}<a_{3}\leq a_{4}\leq q
such that
d_{1}(uv_{i})
is on
\ell(p_{a_{1}},p_{a_{2}})
,
d_{1}(uv_{i+1})
is on
\ell(p_{a_{3}},p_{a_{4}})
and
d_{1}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})=(d_{1}(uv_{i}),p_{a_{2}},\ldots p_{a_{3}},d_{1}(uv_{i+1}))
(if
d_{1}(uv_{i})=p_{a_{2}}
or
d_{1}(uv_{i+1})=p_{a_{3}}
, then omit
p_{a_{2}}
or
p_{a_{3}}
from
d_{1}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
, respectively). Now, since
d
is a
G^{*}
-drawing,
d(\{u,v\})\in{\cal P}^{*}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
, and it is easy to see that
d_{1}(uv_{i+1})\in{\cal P}^{*}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
. So, Condition
3
is satisfied.

Since
d_{1}
obtained by the end of Line
2
is a
G^{*}
-drawing, and
d_{1}
in the end of the algorithm is obtained from
d
by deleting edges and vertices, and renaming points on edges, then it follows that Conditions
4
,
5
and
6
are satisfied.

In conclusion, we proved that the conditions of Definition
38
are satisfied, so
d_{1}
is a
G^{*}
-drawing.
∎

Next, we prove that
d_{1}
returned by Algorithm
2
is a drawing of
\mathsf{IndInfFra}(d_{1},f_{1}(c))
.

Lemma 77
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
d
be a drawing of
F
and let
c
be a cutter of
f
. Let
d_{1}
and
F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}})
be the output of
\mathsf{SplitterPart}_{1}(F,d,

c)
. Then,
d_{1}
is a drawing of
F_{1}
.

Proof.

We need to show that the conditions of Definition
50
are satisfied. Observe that Conditions
1
-
4
are trivially satisfied due to the definition of a frame induced by a
G^{*}
-drawing (Definition
73
). We prove now that Condition
5
holds. Let
\{u,v\}\in E
such that
u,v\in U_{f_{1}}
and
V(d_{f_{1}})\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
. We aim to prove that
\{u,v\}\in E(d_{1})
. This can be shown similarly to the proof of Condition
2(b)iii
of Definition
37
in Lemma
76
. Now, we show that Condition
6
holds. Let
uv_{i}\in V(d_{f_{1}})\cap V^{*}
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
and let
z\in\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i+1}\}
such that
\{z,uv_{i}\}\in E(d_{1})
. Observe that
\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})=p_{1}
, where
E(d_{1})=(d(uv_{i}),p_{1},\ldots,d(z))
, so
\ell(\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}),d_{f}(uv_{i}))
is on
d_{1}(\{uv_{i},z\})
. Thus, Condition
6
holds. Therefore, the conditions of Definition
50
are satisfied, so
d_{1}
is a drawing of
F_{1}
.
∎

Now, we show that
C=(c,F_{1},F_{2})
returned by the
\mathsf{Splitter}
function is an info-cutter of
F
, by showing that the conditions of Definition
71
are satisfied. We begin by proving that
C
is an
Info-Cutter Template
,
U_{f}
-Partitioned
and exhibits the
Equality of Common Parts
property with respect to
F
in Lemmas
78
and
79
and Observation
80
, respectively.

Lemma 78
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
d
be a drawing of
F
and let
c
be a cutter of
f
. Let
C=\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
. Then,
C
is an
Info-Cutter Template
with respect to
F
.

Proof.

Let
F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}})
and
F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}})
.
First,
c
is a cutter of
f
by our assumption. In addition, in Observation
74
we saw that
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are info-frames. So, by Definition
59
,
C
is an
Info-Cutter Template
with respect to
C
.
∎

Lemma 79
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
d
be a drawing of
F
and let
c
be a cutter of
f
. Let
C=\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
. Then,
C
is
U_{f}
-Partitioned
with respect to
F
.

Proof.

Let
F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}})
and
F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}})
.
We show that
C
is
U_{f}
-Partitioned
with respect to
F
(see Definition
62
). Observe that during the course of
\mathsf{SplitterPart}1
(see Algorithm
2
) we do not change the drawing of vertices from the set
V
in
d_{1}
(and, respectively,
d_{2}
with respect to
\mathsf{SplitterPart}2
). Therefore, for every vertex
u\in U_{f}
, that is, a vertex from the set
V
drawn strictly inside
f
, we have that exactly one of the following conditions is satisfied:

•

u
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
. Then,
u\in U_{f_{1}}
.

•

u
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
. Then,
u\in U_{f_{2}}
.

•

u
is drawn on
c
. Observe that, since the first and last vertices of
c
are drawn on
f
,
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\cap\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)\neq\emptyset
. So, since
u
is drawn strictly inside
f
and also on
c
,
d(u)=p
for some
p\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
. Therefore, since
d_{f_{1}}
is a drawing on
f_{1}(c)
,
u\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(

f))
.

Thus, we conclude that
U_{f}=(V(d_{1},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)))\cup U_{f_{1}}\cup U_{f_{2}}
(Condition
1
of Definition
62
). In addition, it is easy to see that
U_{f_{1}}\cap U_{f_{2}}=\emptyset
(Condition
2
of Definition
62
). So,
C
is
U_{f}
-Partitioned
with respect to
F
.
∎

Observe that, by the definition of
\mathsf{SplitterPart}1
(see Algorithm
2
), for every
u\in(V(d)\cap V)\cup(V(d_{1})\cap V)
drawn inside
f_{1}(c)
,
d(u)=d_{1}(u)
. Moreover, for every
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E(d)
and
p\in d(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
inside
d_{1}(c)
, there exists
\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}\in E(d_{1})
such that
p\in d_{1}(\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\})
, and vice versa. In particular,
d_{f}=_{\mathsf{rename}}^{\mathsf{Common}}(f,f_{1})d_{f_{1}}
, where

\mathsf{Common}(f,f_{1})=\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)\cap\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f_{1}(c))
.

Similarly,
d_{f}=_{\mathsf{rename}}^{\mathsf{Common}(f,f_{2})}d_{f_{2}}
, where

\mathsf{Common}

(f,f_{2})=\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)\cap\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f_{2}(c))
, and

d_{f_{1}}=_{\mathsf{rename}}^{\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)}d_{f_{2}}
. Therefore, the conditions of Definition
60
are satisfied. So, we have the following observation:

Observation 80
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
d
be a drawing of
F
and let
c
be a cutter of
f
. Let
C=\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
. Then,
C
exhibits
Equality of Common Parts
with respect to
F
.

Now, we prove that
C
exhibits
Equality of
V^{*}
Directions
with respect to
F
(Definition
61
):

Lemma 81
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
d
be a drawing of
F
and let
c
be a cutter of
f
. Let
C=\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
. Then,
C
exhibits
Equality of
V^{*}
Directions
with respect to
F
.

Proof.

We show that the conditions of Definition
61
are satisfied.

Let
uv_{i}\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))\cap V^{*}
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
. Observe that, similarly to the proof of Lemma
76
, it can be shown that there exists
\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}\in E(d)
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(d(\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\}))
and
(d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}),\{u,v\})
is a turning point in both
f_{1}(c)
and
f_{2}(c)
in
d
. Moreover, let
d(\{uv_{j},uv_{j+1}\})=(p_{1},\ldots p_{q})
. Let
a,b\in\mathbb{N}
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})
is on
\ell(p_{b},p_{b+1})
.

Since
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}

(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
, then
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\neq p_{b},p_{b+1}
. In addition, exactly one among
\ell(p_{b},d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}))
and
\ell(d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}),p_{b+1})
is inside
f_{1}(c)
and the other one is inside
f_{2}(c)
. Therefore, one among
\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}})(uv_{i})
and
\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}(\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i}))
is
p_{b}
and the other one is
p_{b+1}
. It is easy to see that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})
is on
\ell(p_{b},p_{b+1})
. Now, let
uv_{i}\in V(d_{f_{1}},\mathsf{Common}(f,f_{1}))\cap V^{*}
, where
\mathsf{Common}(f,f_{1})=\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)\cap\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(

f_{1}(c))
such that
d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
. Observe that,
\ell(d_{f_{1}}(

uv_{i}),\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})
is on the first edge of
d_{1}(uv_{i},z)
or the first edge of
d_{1}(uv_{i},z)
is on
\ell(d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}),

\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}(uv_{i})
, where
z\in\{uv_{i-1},uv_{i+1}\}
is the neighbor of
uv_{i}
in
d_{1}
. Now, the first edge of
d_{1}(uv_{i},z)
is on the first edge of
d(\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f}}(uv_{i})),z^{\prime})
, where
z^{\prime}
is the neighbor of

d(\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f}}(uv_{i})
in
d
. In addition, since
d
is a drawing of
F
,
\ell(d_{f}(\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f}}(uv_{i})),

\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f}(\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f}}(uv_{i})))
is on the first edge of
d(\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f}}(uv_{i})),z^{\prime})
or vice versa. Therefore, we get that
\ell(d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}))
is on

\ell(d_{f}(\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f}}(uv_{i})),

\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f}(\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f}}(uv_{i})))
or vice versa. Similarly, we can show that this condition holds also for every
uv_{i}\in V(d_{f_{2}},\mathsf{Common}(f,f_{2}))\cap V^{*}
, where
\mathsf{Common}(f,

f_{2})=\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)\cap\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f_{2}(c))
such that

d_{f_{2}}(uv_{i})\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
. Thus, we get that
C
exhibits
Equality of
V^{*}
Directions
.
∎

We continue with the proof that
C
is an info-cutter of
F
. Now, we prove that
C
exhibits
Equality of Common Parts
with respect to
F
.
Towards this, we prove that
C
exhibits
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
with respect to
F
.

Lemma 82
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
d
be a drawing of
F
and let
c
be a cutter of
f
. Let
C=\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
. Then,
C
exhibits the
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
property with respect to
F
.

Proof.

We prove that
C
exhibits
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
property with respect to
F
by showing that the conditions of Definition
63
hold.
Let
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E_{f}
such that
uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\in V(d_{f})
. Since
d
is a drawing of
F
,
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
is an edge that drawn strictly inside
d
except at its endpoints. Then, exactly one of the following conditions is satisfied:

1.

The edge
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
except at its endpoints (e.g., see
\{uv_{3},uv_{4}\}
in Figure
23(a)
). Then,
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i}),

\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E_{1}
, so Condition
1
is satisfied. Observe that
(d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i}),\{u,v\})
and
(d_{f_{1}}(uv_{i+1}),\{u,v\})
are turning points in
f_{1}(c)
. Thus, there exists
uv_{j}\in V(d_{f_{1}})\cap V^{*}
such that
d_{f}(uv_{i})=d_{f_{1}}(uv_{j})
, so
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i})\neq\mathsf{Null}
. Similarly, we have that
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i+1})\neq\mathsf{Null}
.

2.

The edge
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
except at its endpoints (e.g., see
\{a,b\}
in Figure
23(a)
). Then,
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}(uv_{i})},

\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E_{2}
, so Condition
2
is satisfied. Similarly to the previous case, we have that
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i})\neq\mathsf{Null}
and
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})\neq\mathsf{Null}
.

3.

The edge
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
is drawn on
c
, so
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{f,d_{1}^{*}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{f,d_{1}^{*}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E(d_{1}^{*},

\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
, and hence Condition
3
is satisfied.

4.

Otherwise, we show that
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
partly intersects
c
(see Definition
64
) (e.g., see
\{uv_{1},uv_{2}\}
in Figure
23(a)
). Let
r\in[2]

such that
uv_{i}
is drawn on
f_{r}(c)
in
d
. Observe that there must be
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
points
p_{i}\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
such that
p_{i}
is on
d(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
, and
(p_{i},\{u,v\})
is a turning point of
f_{r}(c)
in
d
. Now, in
d_{r}
(returned by
\mathsf{SplitterPart}_{1}
), on each of these points are drawn vertices from
V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}
, enumerated in an increasing order from
uv_{i}
to
uv_{i+1}
. Let
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{r}}}(uv_{i})=uv_{j}
. Now, for every
0\leq t<\ell
, since
(d_{r}(uv_{j+t}),\{u,v\})
is a turning point in
f_{r}(c)
, exactly one of the following conditions is satisfied.

•

The part of the edge
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
from the point
d_{r}(uv_{j+t})
to the point
d_{r}(uv_{j+t+1})
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
except at the points
d_{r}(uv_{j+t})
and
d_{r}(uv_{j+t+1})
.
Now, if
r=1
, then
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E_{1}
; else,
r=2
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{f_{d_{2}},f_{d_{1}}}(uv_{j+t}),

\mathsf{Identify}_{f_{d_{2}},f_{d_{1}}}

(uv_{j+t+1})\}\in E_{1}
. So, Condition
1
is satisfied.

•

Similarly, the part of the edge
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
from the point
d_{r}(uv_{j+t})
to the point
d_{r}(uv_{j+t+1})
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
except at the points
d_{r}(uv_{j+t})
and

d_{r}(uv_{j+t+1})
.
If
r=2
, then
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E_{2}
; else,
r=1
, and then
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{f_{d_{1}},f_{d_{2}}}

(uv_{j+t}),

\mathsf{Identify}_{f_{d_{1}},f_{d_{2}}}

(uv_{j+t+1})\}\in E_{2}
. So, Condition
2
is satisfied.

•

The part of the edge
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
from the point
d_{r}(uv_{j+t})
to the point
d_{r}(uv_{j+t+1})
is drawn on
c
. In this case, it follows that
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E(d_{r}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
. So, Condition
3
is satisfied.

Similarly, it can be shown that exactly one of Conditions
4
-
6
is satisfied.
Then, the conditions of Definition
64
hold, so
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
partly intersects
c
.

In conclusion, we proved that all conditions of Definition
63
hold, so
C
exhibits the
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
property with respect to
F
.
∎

Similarly to Lemma
82
, it can be proved that
C
exhibits the
Partition of
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
and
Partition of
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
properties with respect to
F
. We state this in the next lemma (without providing a proof):

Lemma 83
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
d
be a drawing of
F
and let
c
be a cutter of
f
. Let
C=\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
. Then,
C
exhibits the
Partition of
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
and
Partition of
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
properties with respect to
F
.

Now, we prove that
C
exhibits the
Partition of
\{u,v\}\in E
Drawn Strictly Inside
f
property with respect to
F
.

Lemma 84
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
d
be a drawing of
F
and let
c
be a cutter of
f
. Let
C=\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
. Then,
C
exhibits
Partition of
\{u,v\}\in E
Drawn Strictly Inside
f
property with respect to
F
.

Proof.

We show that the conditions of Definition
69
holds. Let
u,v\in U_{f}
such that
\{u,v\}\in E
and
V(d_{f})\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
. Since
d
is a drawing of
F
, it follows that
\{u,v\}
is drawn strictly inside
f
in
d
. Then, exactly one of the following conditions holds:

1.

\{u,v\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
. Therefore,
V(f_{d_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
and
u,v\in U_{1}
; so, Condition
1
is satisfied.

2.

\{u,v\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}(c)
. Therefore,
V(f_{d_{2}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
and
u,v\in U_{2}
; so, Condition
2
is satisfied.

3.

\{u,v\}
is drawn on
c
. Therefore
V(f_{d_{1}},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c))\cap V^{*}_{\{u,v\}}=\emptyset
and
\{u,v\}\in E(f_{d_{1}},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
; so, Condition
3
is satisfied.

4.

Otherwise, we aim to prove that
\{u,v\}
partly intersects
c
(see Definition
70
). There are
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
points
p_{i}\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
, such that
(p_{i},\{u,v\})
is a turning point in
f_{1}(c)
. It can be shown, similarly to Condition
4
in the proof of Lemma
82
, that for every
1\leq t<\ell
, exactly one of Conditions
1
-
3
, exactly one of Conditions
4
-
8
and exactly one of Conditions
9
-
13
are satisfied. So, Condition
4
is satisfied.

Therefore,
C
exhibits the
Partition of
\{u,v\}\in E
Drawn Strictly Inside
f
property with respect to
F
.
∎

In conclusion, in Lemmas
78
-
84
and in Observation
80
we saw that
\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
is an
Info-Cutter Template
and
U_{f}
-Partitioned
with respect to
F
. In addition,
\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
exhibits the
Equality of
V^{*}
Directions
,
Equality of Common Parts
,
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
,
Partition of
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
,
Partition of
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
and
Partition of
\{u,v\}\in E
Drawn Strictly Inside
f
properties with respect to
F
. Therefore, by Definition
71
,
\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
is info-cutter of
F
. Moreover, in Lemma
77
, we saw that
\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{d_{1}}
and
\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{d_{2}}
are drawings of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
, respectively, where
\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}=(c,F_{1},F_{2})
.
So, we have the following lemma:

Lemma 85
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
d
be a drawing of
F
and let
c
be a cutter of
f
. Then,
\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}
is info-cutter of
F
. In addition,
\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{d_{1}}
and
\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{d_{2}}
are drawings of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
, respectively, where
\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,c)_{C}=(c,F_{1},F_{2})
.

Now, for a later use (in Section
6.8
), we present another observation. Let
f^{\prime}
be a frame that is bounded by
f_{1}(c)
. Observe that the part of the drawing
d
intersected by
f^{\prime}
is equal to the part of the drawing
d_{1}
intersected by
f^{\prime}
, up to renaming. Thus, we have the following claims. The subset of vertices from
V
that intersect
f^{\prime}
in
d
equals the subset of vertices from the set
V
that intersect
f^{\prime}
in
d_{1}
. Moreover, every turning point
(p,\{u,v\})
in
f^{\prime}
in
d
is a turning point in
d_{1}
, and vice versa. Therefore, we get that
\mathsf{cost}(f^{\prime},d)=\mathsf{cost}(f^{\prime},d_{1})
, as we state in the next observation:

Observation 86
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
C=(c,F_{1},F_{2})
be an info-cutter of
F
, let
d
be a drawing of
F
, and let
f^{\prime}
be a frame that is bounded by
f_{1}(c)
. Then,
\mathsf{cost}(f^{\prime},d)=\mathsf{cost}(f^{\prime},d_{1})
, where
d_{1}=\mathsf{Splitter}(F,d,C)_{d_{1}}
.

6.6
The Function
\mathsf{Glue}

Now, we introduce the “inverse function” of the
\mathsf{Splitter}
function from Section
6.5
, called the
\mathsf{Glue}
function. This function gets as input an info-frame
F
, an info-cutter
C=(c,F_{1},F_{2})
of
F
, a drawing
d_{1}
of
F_{1}
and a drawing
d_{2}
of
F_{2}
. The purpose of this function is to “glue” the drawings
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
, and return as output a drawing of
F
(see Figure
24
). The way we glue the two drawings is very intuitive, due to the definition of an info-cutter explained in Section
6.3
.

(a)

(b)

(c)

Figure 24
:
An illustration of the
\mathsf{Glue}
function. The vertices mapped to points in
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f)\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f)
,
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{1})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f_{1})
and
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{2})\setminus\mathsf{GridPointSet}(f_{2})
are denoted by hollow squares. (a) Drawings of the info-frames of
f_{1}(c)
and
f_{2}(c)
, described in Figure
23(e)
. A cutter
c
of
f
is colored green. (b) The figure showing the intuition behind the Glue function. The drawing shown is obtained by merging the common vertices of
f_{1}(c)
and
f_{2}(c)
together. (c) The drawing
d
of
F
obtained by
\mathsf{Glue}
from the figures in (a).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},

\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),

F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
be an info-cutter of
F
, and let
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
be drawings of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
, respectively.
\mathsf{Glue}(F,C,d_{1},d_{2})
will be a pair of functions
d=(d_{\mathsf{V}},d_{\mathsf{E}})
(defined in Definition
90
). First, we define
d_{\mathsf{V}}
by
\mathsf{GlueVer}(F,C,d_{1},d_{2})
as follows:

Definition 87

(
\mathsf{GlueVer}
).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}})
,
F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
be an info-cutter of
F
, and let
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
be drawings of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
, respectively. Then,
\mathsf{GlueVer}(F,C,d_{1},d_{2})=d_{\mathsf{V}}
is the function defined as follows:

1.

For every
v\in V(d_{f})
,
d_{\mathsf{V}}(v)=d_{f}(v)
.

2.

For every
v\in U_{f}
, if
v\in V(d_{1})
, then
d_{\mathsf{V}}(v)=d_{1}(v)
; else,
d_{\mathsf{V}}(v)=d_{2}(v)
.

Next, we define the function
d_{\mathsf{E}}
by
\mathsf{GlueEdg}(F,C,d_{1},d_{2})
. Here, the definitions for the drawings of the edges correlate with
the properties
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
,
Partition of
\{u,uv_{1}\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
,

Partition of
\{uv_{\mathsf{index}(u,v)},v\}\in E_{f}
With One Endpoint on
f
and
Partition of
\{u,v\}\in E
Drawn Strictly Inside
f
(see Definitions
63
,
65
,
67
and
69
, respectively). So, for the sake of readability, we split the definition for the drawings of the edges into separate definitions, according to these properties.

Definition 88

(
\mathsf{GlueEdg}
).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}})

F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
be an info-cutter of
F
, and let
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
be drawings of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
, respectively. Then,
\mathsf{GlueEdg}(F,C,d_{1},d_{2})=d_{\mathsf{E}}
is the function defined as follows:

1.

For every
e\in E(d_{f})
,
d_{\mathsf{E}}(e)=d_{f}(e)

Condition

In this case,

(Condition
3
Definition
64
).

We obtain a drawing for each part similarly to Conditions

Definition 89

(
\mathsf{Glue}
of

Let

of

(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})\})
.

=d_{f_{1}}^{*}(\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}^{*}})

\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}=d_{2}(\{uv_{j+\ell},uv_{j+\ell+1}\})

.

(f_{\mathsf{init}})

.

The other definitions, for the drawings of the edges in Conditions

Now, we are ready to define the drawing
\mathsf{Glue}
.

Definition 90

(
\mathsf{Glue}
).

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},E_{f_{1}},U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}})
,
F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
be an info-cutter of
F
, and let
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
be drawings of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
, respectively. Then,
\mathsf{Glue}(F,C,d_{1},d_{2})=(\mathsf{GlueVer}(F,C,d_{1},

d_{2}),\mathsf{GlueEdg}(F,C,d_{1},d_{2}))
.

Recall that our goal is to define a drawing
d
of
F
. But first, observe that we use
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
in order to define
d=(d_{\mathsf{V}},d_{\mathsf{E}})
(see Definitions
87
and
88
). Each time we use
d_{1}
or
d_{2}
for a certain vertex or edge, we assume that this vertex or edge belongs to the domain of
d_{1}
or
d_{2}
, respectively. These assumptions are valid due to the info-cutter properties (see Section
6.4
), as we will immediately show. Specifically, we say that definitions are
well defined
if the aforementioned assumptions are valid, and we begin by proving that
d_{\mathsf{V}}
is well defined:

Lemma 91
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},

E_{f_{1}},

U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}})

F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
be an info-cutter of
F
, and let
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
be drawings of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
, respectively. Then,
\mathsf{GlueVer}(F,C,d_{1},d_{2})=d_{\mathsf{V}}
(see Definition
87
) is well defined.

Proof.

In Condition
1
, for every
u\in V(d_{f})
,
d_{\mathsf{V}}(u)=d_{f}(u)
, so
u
is clearly in the domain of
d_{f}
.

In Condition
2
, for every
u\in U_{f}
, if
u\in V(d_{1})
, then
d_{\mathsf{V}}(u)=d_{1}(u)
, so
d_{\mathsf{V}}(u)
is well defined in this case.

If
u\notin V(d_{1})
, then
d_{\mathsf{V}}(u)=d_{2}(u)
. We show that
d_{\mathsf{V}}(u)
is well defined in this case as well.
Observe that since
C
is an info-cutter of
F
, then
C
is
U_{f}
-Partitioned
with respect to
F
. Therefore, by Condition
1
of Definition
62
,
U_{f}=V(d_{1},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f))\cup U_{1}\cup U_{2}
. Now, since
d_{1}
is a drawing of
F_{1}
, then, by Condition
3
of Definition
50
,
U_{1}
is the set of vertices drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
. So,
u\notin V(d_{1})
implies
u\notin U_{1}
. In addition,
u\notin V(d_{1})
implies
u\notin V(d_{1},\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f))
, and then,
u\in U_{2}
. Now, since
d_{2}
is a drawing of
F_{2}
, again by Condition
3
of Definition
50
,
U_{2}
is the set of vertices drawn strictly inside
f_{1}(c)
in
d_{2}
, so
u\in V(d_{2})
.

Therefore,
d_{\mathsf{V}}
is well defined.
∎

Now, we aim to prove that
d_{\mathsf{E}}
is well defined. In particular, we show that the drawings of the edges in Definition
89
are well defined; the proof for the other edges (corresponding to Conditions
2b
,
2c
and
3
in Definition
88
) is similar. We also prove (in the next lemma) three additional properties that will come in handy when we prove that
d
is a drawing of
F
.

Lemma 92
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},

E_{f_{1}},

U_{f_{1}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}})
,
F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
be an info-cutter of
F
, and let
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
drawings of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
, respectively. Let
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E_{f}
such that
uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\in V(d_{f})
. Then, the following conditions are satisfied:

1.

\mathsf{Glue}
of
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
is well defined.

2.

d_{\mathsf{E}}(uv_{i},uv_{i+1})\in{\cal P}^{*}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
.

3.

d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
is drawn strictly inside
f
except for its endpoints.

4.

The endpoints of
d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
are
d_{\mathsf{V}}(uv_{i})
and
d_{\mathsf{V}}(uv_{i+1})
defined in Definition
87
.

Proof.

We prove the satisfaction of the first three conditions. It easy to see, in each of the following cases, that Condition
4
is satisfied as well, due to the definition of
d_{\mathsf{V}}
in Definition
87
. Now, since
C
is an info-cutter of
F
,
C
exhibits the
Partition of
E_{f}
With Both Endpoints on
f
property with respect to
F
. Therefore, exactly one of the following conditions is satisfied.

1.

\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E_{f_{1}}
. In this case, since
d_{1}
is a drawing of
F_{1}
,
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E(d_{1})
. Thus,
d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
is well defined, and
d_{\mathsf{E}}(uv_{i},uv_{i+1})\in{\cal P}^{*}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
, so Conditions
1
and
2
are satisfied. In addition, again since
d_{1}
is a drawing of
F_{1}
and
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i}),

\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E_{f_{1}}
, we have that
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}

(uv_{i+1})\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{1}
except for its endpoints, which are drawn on
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)\cap\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f_{1}(c))
in
d_{1}
. So,
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
is drawn strictly inside
f
except for its endpoints, which are drawn on
f
. Thus, Condition
3
is satisfied.

2.

\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E_{f_{2}}
. In this case, since
d_{2}
is a drawing of
F_{2}
,
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E(d_{2})
. Thus,
d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
is well defined, and
d_{\mathsf{E}}(uv_{i},uv_{i+1})\in{\cal P}^{*}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
, so Conditions
1
and
2
are satisfied. In addition, again since
d_{2}
is a drawing of
F_{2}
and
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i}),

\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E_{f_{2}}
, we have that
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}

(uv_{i+1})\}
is drawn strictly inside
f_{2}
except for its endpoints, which are drawn on
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)\cap\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f_{2}(c))
in
d_{2}
. So,
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
is drawn strictly inside
f
except for its endpoints, which are drawn on
f
. Thus, Condition
3
is satisfied.

3.

Since
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{f,d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(uv_{i}),\mathsf{Identify}_{f,d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E(d_{f_{1}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
,
d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})

=d_{f_{1}}^{*}(\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}^{*}})
is well defined and
d_{\mathsf{E}}(uv_{i},uv_{i+1})\in{\cal P}^{*}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
. In addition,

\{\mathsf{Identify}_{f,d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(uv_{i}),

\mathsf{Identify}_{f,d_{f_{1}}^{*}}(uv_{i+1})\}\in E(d_{f_{1}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
implies that
d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},

uv_{i+1}\})
is drawn strictly inside
f
except for its endpoints. Thus, Conditions
1
,
2
and
3
are satisfied.

4.

Otherwise,
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
partly intersects
c
. Then, let
\ell,j\in\mathbb{N}
and
r\in[2]
be as defined in Definition
64
. We show that
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}
is well defined, for every
0\leq t<\ell
, according to the following different cases:

(a)

If
r=1
and
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E_{f_{1}}
, then
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}=d_{1}(\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\})
; if

and
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+t}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+t+1})\}\in E_{1}
, then
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}=d_{1}(\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+t}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+t+1})\})
. In both cases, similarly to the case shown in Condition
1
, it follows that
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}
is well defined.

(b)

If
r=2
and
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E_{f_{2}}
, then
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}=d_{2}(\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\})
; if
r=1
and
\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+t}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+t+1})\}\in E_{2}
, then
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}=d_{2}(\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+t}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+t+1})\})
. In both cases, similarly to the case shown in Condition
2
, it follows that
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}
is well defined.

(c)

If
\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\}\in E(d_{f_{r}}^{*},\mathsf{PlanePoints}(c))
, then
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}=d_{f_{r}}^{*}(\{uv_{j+t},uv_{j+t+1}\})
, so it is easy to see that
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}
is well defined.

Now, we examine
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}
according to the following different cases:

(d)

If
r=1
and
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i+1})=uv_{j+\ell+1}
, then
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}=d_{1}(\{uv_{j+\ell},uv_{j+\ell+1}\})
; else,
r=2
and then
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}=d_{1}(\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{2}},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{j+\ell}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{1}}}(uv_{i+1})\})
. In both cases, similarly to the case shown in Condition
1
, it follows that
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}
is well defined.

(e)

If
r=2
and
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})=uv_{j+\ell+1}
, then
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}=d_{2}(\{uv_{j+\ell},uv_{j+\ell+1}\})
; else,
r=1
and then
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}=d_{2}(\{\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f_{1}},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{j+\ell}),\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{2}}}(uv_{i+1})\})
. In both cases, similarly to the case shown in Condition
2
, it follows that
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}
is well defined.

(f)

Otherwise,
\mathsf{Identify}_{d_{f},d_{f_{r}}}(uv_{i+1})=uv_{j+\ell+1}
and then
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}=d_{f_{r}}^{*}(\{uv_{j+\ell},uv_{j+\ell+1}\}

)
, so it is easy to see that
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}
is well defined.

Now, it is easy to see that for every for every
0\leq t<\ell
, if
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}=(p_{1},\ldots p_{k})
and
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t+1}=(q_{1},\ldots q_{s})
, then
p_{k}=q_{1}
. So,
P=\mathsf{PartEdge}_{0}\cdot\mathsf{PartEdge}_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}
is well defined, and thus
d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
is well defined. In conclusion, we get that Condition
1
is satisfied.

Moreover, recall that
d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
is the path obtained from
P=\mathsf{PartEdge}_{0}\cdot\mathsf{PartEdge}_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{q})
by deleting every

p_{i}\in\mathsf{EnrichedGridPointsIn}(f_{\mathsf{init}})\setminus\mathsf{StrictGridPoints}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
where
i\neq 1,q
. Therefore, it is easy to see that
d_{\mathsf{E}}(uv_{i},uv_{i+1})\in{\cal P}^{*}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
, so Condition
2
is satisfied.

In addition, consider the following observations:

•

\mathsf{PartEdge}_{0}
, similarly to Conditions
1
,
2
and
3
, is drawn strictly inside
f
, except at one of the endpoints: one of the endpoints is drawn on

\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f_{r})\cap\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
(and therefore drawn on
f
), and the other is drawn on
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
(and therefore strictly inside
f
).

•

Similarly, for every
0<t<\ell
,
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{t}
is drawn strictly inside
f
, as both of its endpoints are drawn on
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
(and therefore strictly inside
f
).

•

\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}
, similarly to
\mathsf{PartEdge}_{0}
, is drawn strictly inside
f
, except at one of the endpoints: one of the endpoints is drawn on is drawn on
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(c)\setminus\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
(and therefore strictly inside
f
), and the other is drawn on
\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f_{r})\cap\mathsf{EnrichedGridPoints}(f)
(and therefore drawn on
f
).

Finally, we get that
P=\mathsf{PartEdge}_{0}\cdot\mathsf{PartEdge}_{1}\cdot\ldots\cdot\mathsf{PartEdge}_{\ell}
is strictly inside
f
, except for both of its endpoints, and therefore also
d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
, so Condition
3
is satisfied.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

The following lemma, Lemma
93
, is the lemma analogous to Lemma
92
, corresponding to the drawings of edges defined in Conditions
2b
and
2c
of Definition
88
. Observe that, in Condition
3
of Lemma
93
, only one endpoint of the edge is drawn on
d_{f}
, as opposed to Condition
3
of Lemma
92
. Since Lemma
93
can be proved similarly to Lemma
92
, we do not provide a proof.

Lemma 93
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},

E_{f_{1}},

U_{f_{1}},

\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
be an info-cutter of
F
, and let
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
be drawings of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
, respectively. Let
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}\in E_{f}
such that exactly one among
uv_{i}
and
uv_{i+1}
is drawn on
d_{f}
. Then, the following conditions are satisfied:

1.

\mathsf{Glue}
of
\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\}
is well defined.

2.

d_{\mathsf{E}}(uv_{i},uv_{i+1})\in{\cal P}^{*}(f_{\mathsf{init}})
.

3.

d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
is drawn strictly inside
f
except for exactly one of its endpoints.

4.

The endpoints of
d_{\mathsf{E}}(\{uv_{i},uv_{i+1}\})
are
d_{\mathsf{V}}(uv_{i})
and
d_{\mathsf{V}}(uv_{i+1})
, defined in Definition
87
.

Similarly, the following lemma is analogous to Lemma
92
, corresponding to the drawings of edges defined in Condition
3
of Definition
88
. In Condition
3
of this lemma, the edge
\{u,v\}
is drawn strictly inside
f
.

Lemma 94
.

Let
F=(f,d_{f},E_{f},U_{f},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f})
be an info-frame, let
C=(c,F_{1}=(f_{1}(c),d_{f_{1}},

E_{f_{1}},

U_{f_{1}},

\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{1}}),F_{2}=(f_{2}(c),d_{f_{2}},E_{f_{2}},U_{f_{2}},\mathsf{V^{*}Dir}_{f_{2}}))
be an info-cutter of
F
, and let
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
be drawings of
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
